<summary>This is the title page for a comprehensive three-part index to the works of the famous mystic Jacob Böhme, designed to help readers navigate his specialized vocabulary and scriptural interpretations. Published in 1730, it reflects the enduring popularity of Böhme's theosophy over a century after his death.</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Böhme, Theosophy, Index, Mysticism, Reference Work, Biblical Commentary, 18th-century Printing</keywords>

<meta>This page begins the preface (Vorbericht) to a collection of Jacob Böhme's works, likely the 1730 or similar collected edition. It introduces the supplementary indexes provided for the reader's benefit.</meta>

An ornate rectangular woodcut headpiece featuring a symmetrical and dense composition of scrolling vines, leaves, and various flowers.

# Preface
## to the Lover of these Noble Writings.

The God-loving reader <note>A traditional 17th-century greeting to the pious reader.</note> receives herewith a triple index for all the theosophical <term>theosophical: Referring to "Divine Wisdom," a branch of mystical philosophy seeking direct knowledge of the nature of God and the created world.</term> writings of the blessed and highly-enlightened Jacob Böhme <note>Jacob Böhme (1575–1624) was a German mystic and master shoemaker whose visionary writings on the nature of God, light, and darkness deeply influenced Western philosophy and Christian mysticism.</note>; these have not only been diligently revised and improved for this new edition, but have also, through God’s goodness, been augmented with many beautiful additions. And since this work—given its manifold usefulness—cannot be anything but pleasant and joyful to the reader, we have likewise felt encouraged to provide him with sufficient information regarding the arrangement of these indexes and everything else that might be noteworthy therein.

2. The first index is a **VOCABULARY**, or a thorough explanation of all Magic-Mystical <term>Terms</term> <note>original: "Terminorum," the Latin plural for "terms."</note> which the author uses in his writings, especially according to the meaning of the Spirit; additionally, other occurring Latin words have not been omitted, for the sake of those who are not familiar with that language. Regarding the first kind, God has [given] the reader an incompar- <note>The word is cut off by the page break; the catchword "ver-" suggests "unvergleichliche" or "incomparable."</note>

<summary>This preface introduces a new edition of Jacob Böhme's mystical writings, highlighting the inclusion of three specialized indexes designed to help readers navigate his complex terminology and spiritual concepts.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, theosophy, mysticism, vocabulary, spiritual terminology, 17th-century literature, religious indexes</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the "Vorbericht" or Preface from page 3, specifically finishing the description of the first index (the Vocabulary) and beginning the description of the second index (the Index of Scripture Passages).</meta>

->Preface.<-

-able and very noble addition <note>Continuing from the previous page: "unver-muthete" or "unexpected."</note>, which, having been unjustly withheld from the Index of 1715, has now been entrusted to this current one again, to animate it as if with the pearl of the heavenly Virgin <note>Sophia, or Divine Wisdom, a central figure in Böhme’s mysticism representing the mirror of God.</note>, as a seal of the Holy Spirit, so that the reader shall now find a pure spirit with God in the Word. All these newly entered passages are marked with an asterisk (*), as a notice to the God-loving reader; and just as love was born in *Sophia* <note>Divine Wisdom</note> through this work, bringing the Spirit of Christ into it so that readers might be turned from the letter to the soul <note>original: "Gemüth," referring to the inner mind, disposition, or heart.</note> and lifted out of darkness into the light: so She <note>Wisdom/Sophia</note> has given herself to those souls who hunger for God, to unlock their hearts, that they may be awakened to the joy and praise of God. For it is a fixed word that reveals itself within as a power of God. The reader will also find all the most important words together in the third index, and alongside them the detailed explanation of the blessed J. B. <note>Jacob Böhme.</note> in the cited passages. Furthermore, great care has been taken to explain all other words more clearly and correctly wherever it was necessary.

3. The second index is an **Index of Passages of the Holy Scriptures** <note>original: "INDEX LOCORVM S. S."</note> or a register of all the sayings and places in Holy Scripture which the author has either thoroughly explained or merely cited and thereby brought into the light. To the former belongs especially the First Book of Moses <note>The Book of Genesis.</note>, which is explained through every chapter in detail, and in the high light of the spirit, in a dedicated treatise, *The Great Mystery* <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum," Böhme's massive commentary on Genesis.</note>—

->Preface.<-

<note>The text begins mid-sentence, continuing from the previous page's discussion of scriptural citations.</note> ...named and explained by him, and can be found in its proper place within the work. This index has also been diligently reviewed for this new edition, and the incorrect <term>citations</term> <note>original: "allegata"</note> therein have been properly corrected.

4. The third is an index of all the **Theosophical Subject Matters** <note>These are the core spiritual and philosophical topics explored by Böhme, such as the nature of the Divine or the origin of the soul.</note> treated by the author, as well as the most remarkable words and names occurring therein; where the reader may look up every matter under its respective letter and find the entire treatise on the same (as it is to be found in the <term>treatises</term> <note>original: "Tractaten"</note> and their chapters and paragraphs indicated alongside) concentrated, or presented in a clear brevity. This is intended for the significant easing of comprehension and the kindling of knowledge in these high and deep matters, to the heartfelt praise of **GOD**. This index has been thoroughly revised in the best possible way for this new edition; efforts have been made to put everything in its right place and to arrange it in better order. Superfluous or doubly-cited entries have been omitted, and the subject matters—where it could be done without detriment to clarity—have been expressed more concisely. Any encountered <term>errata</term> or <term>printing errors</term> <note>original: "Druck-Fehler"</note>, as well as whatever else was necessary to change, have been diligently improved. The reader will find this to be true upon <term>comparison</term> <note>original: "Conferirung"</note> of this new edition with the edition of the year 1715. Nevertheless, the general arrangement of the index has been maintained for internal reasons <note>original: "gemüthlichen Ursachen," referring to the spiritual or conceptual logic behind the original structure.</note>, though we have chosen to divide the realities occurring under each title into their own distinct paragraphs for greater clarity.

<meta>This page continues the "Vorbericht" or Preface, specifically detailing the enhancements made to the Subject Index. It introduces the addition of marginal notes by Johann Georg Gichtel and explains the standardized abbreviations for Böhme's various treatises.</meta>

-># Preface.<-

5. Furthermore, in this current new edition, this **Subject Index**—no less than the first one, or the so-called **Vocabulary**—has been adorned with a beautiful and lovely little pearl. God has so ordained it that we are able to share with the reader, for their blessing, several extraordinary notes by the late and dearly proven **practitioner of faith** <note>original: "Glaubens-Practici," referring to one who lives out their spiritual convictions through experience rather than just theory</note> regarding these noble spiritual writings: Mr. Johann Georg Gichtel <note>Gichtel (1638–1710) was a famous German mystic and a primary editor/promoter of Jacob Böhme's works; his annotations were highly prized by followers.</note>. These are notes he recorded long ago while reading these works. Those who are connoisseurs, or even just souls who long for God, will find therein the core of the **Theosophical Matters** handled by the author, full of the sweetness and power of the Spirit. Through this, they will be awakened to the praise of God and to gratitude for this unexpected gift of grace in the love of Jesus. These notes have been inserted throughout in their proper places and marked with a preceding asterisk (*), as a pleasant notice for the enthusiast.

6. Moreover, the writings of the author have been listed in the indexes in the very same order as they appear in the work itself, though they have been abbreviated (according to the instructions following hereafter). Where numbers previously appeared alongside a treatise title, these have been omitted to prevent any confusion with the subsequent numbers of chapters and paragraphs. Therefore, one has used, for example <note>original: "z. e." for "zum Exempel"</note>, **Questions on the Soul**, or **Soul-Qu.**, instead of *40 Questions on the Soul*; likewise <note>original: "it." for "item"</note> **Theosophical Points** for *6 Theosophical Points*; furthermore **Mystic Points** for *6 Mystic Points*; and finally **Theosophical Questions** for *177 Theosophical Questions*. Great care has been taken to print the numbers themselves cleanly, clearly, and accurately; and finally, in a

<meta>This page concludes the Preface (Vorbericht), explaining the transition from page-based indexing to chapter-and-paragraph indexing. It provides a key for the abbreviations of Jacob Böhme’s major works used throughout the volume’s reference sections.</meta>

-># Preface.<-

indicated in the following instructions how all the writings are abbreviated when cited in the indexes, so that a beginning reader can find their way more easily.

7. Finally, in the indexes of this new edition, when citing the treatises, instead of the page and section numbers used in the 1715 edition <note>original: "Edit. von Ao. 1715," refers to the previous collected edition of Böhme's works</note>, only the chapters and paragraphs <note>The symbol § is used for paragraphs</note> or sections of each place have been introduced with great diligence. Thus, the first number indicates the chapter and the second the paragraph or section. Consequently, these mentioned indexes—and especially the third one, the **Subject Index** <note>original: "Realien," referring to a "Real Index," which indexes concepts and topics rather than just words or names</note>—can also be used with great benefit for the previous editions, particularly the one from 1682. May the love of God in Christ Jesus grant this to every God-seeking reader for the eternal joy of the spirit!

---

## Instructions,

### On how all the writings have been abbreviated when cited in the indexes.

1. **Aurora**, or **Day-Spring**, appears as either *Aurora* or
-> Aur. <-
2. **Three Principles of Divine Essence**; and **Appendix** = *Princ. et Append.* <note>original Latin: "Principia et Appendix"</note>
-> Princ. and App. <-
3. **The Threefold Life of Man** = *Threef.* or *thr. Life.*
4. **Forty Questions concerning the Soul** = *Soul Ques.* or *Soul*
-> *Fr.* <-
5. **The Incarnation of Jesus Christ** Part I <note>original Latin: "Pars I"</note> = *Incarn.* or *Inc.*
-> *Chr. I.* <-

<page-num>9</page-num>
<header>❊ ):( o ):( ❊</header>
<sig>a 5</sig>

## The First Index.
# VOCABULARY
->or<-
### Explanation
->of the Magical-Mystical Terms<-
->contained in the Author’s writings,<-
->according to the sense of the Spirit;<-
->as well as<-
->other occurring Latin words.<-

->A<-

**Abba**, Father <note>original: "Abba" (Aramaic/Hebrew term for father, often used in the New Testament to signify intimacy with God).</note>.
**Absentia**, absence, the state of being away.
**Absolviren**, to unbind, to speak loose and free, to forgive sin.
**Absolution**, the release, forgiveness of sins.
**Abstinentia**, abstinence, refraining, moderation.
**Academia**, Academy, the high school <note>original: "hohe Schule," a common 17th-century term for a university.</note>, university.
**Academisch**, in the manner of the high schools or universities.
**Accordiren**, to agree, to be of the same mind.
**Adde**, set or add to <note>original: "Adde" (Latin imperative for "add").</note>.
**Adonai**, the Lord of all things; is a Name of God <note>original: "Adonai" (Hebrew for "my Lord").</note>.
**Adumbriren**, to sketch out, to represent a thing in a draft, painting, or also in writing.
**Advocatus**, an advocate, intercessor, legal representative.
**Advociren**, to speak for someone, to defend someone's cause before a court according to the course of the law.
**Aequanimitas**, equanimity, calmness, steadiness of the mind.
**Aequinoctial-Circul**, the supposed path or circle of the sun in the sky when day and night are of equal length, called "the Line" <note>the Equator</note> by seafarers or sailors.
**Aeternitas**, eternity, a certain time according to the Divine Economy <note>In Böhme’s theology, the "Divine Economy" refers to the specific way God organizes and reveals His will through time and eternity.</note>, eternity.
**Aether**, the natural heaven; it is a degree more subtle than the air.
**Affectus**, affections, passions, all kinds of arousal of the mind, inclinations of the soul, desires, passions.
**Affect-los**, free from desires or affections.

<meta>catchword: Affecti</meta>

<summary>This page marks the beginning of a specialized glossary (Vocabularium) defining the mystical, theological, and Latin terms used by Jacob Böhme. It covers entries from "Abba" to "Affect-los," providing both literal translations and spiritual context for his terminology.</summary>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page continues the "First Index" or glossary of terms begun on the previous page. It covers terms from "Affectionate" to "To Appeal," focusing heavily on early scientific (Alchemical, Anatomical) and theological concepts used by Jacob Böhme.</meta>

<page-num>10</page-num>
<header>A. The First Index of</header>

**Affectionate**, favorable, well-disposed.
**Africa**, the 3rd part of the inhabited globe <note>In the 17th century, the world was traditionally divided into three parts: Europe, Asia, and Africa.</note>, containing Egypt and the lands of the Moors, situated toward the south <note>original: "gegen Mittag," literally "toward midday," a common historical term for the South.</note>.
**Alchemy** <term>Alchymia: the art of separating metals through fire, or the art of making gold.</term> It is a hidden science that brings imperfect metals to maturity through their own fire, or transforms lesser metals, such as lead, iron, etc., into genuine gold.
**Alchemist** [Alchymista], such an artist of gold and nature, an alchemist.
**The Alcoran** <note>The Quran</note>, the book of law of the Turks, given to them by Mahomet <note>Muhammad</note>.
**Alexandria**, is a city in Egypt.
**Alphabet** [Alphabetum], the "ABC" in the Greek language, in which *alpha* is the first and *omega* is the last letter. Commonly, this is the name given to the "ABC" of all languages.
**Alumnus**, one who receives discipline, sustenance, and instruction from someone; a pupil.
**Anatomy** [Anatomia], the artistic dissection of corpses, together with a detailed description of all external and internal members of the body.
**Anatomist** [Anatomicus], one who understands and practices such a science.
**To Anatomize**, to artistically dissect dead bodies and lay them out and prepare them for close observation.
**Animalia**, animals, all creatures that have a living soul, like the breath in their nostrils, and can move themselves from place to place.
**Animalish**, soulish, animal-like. The animalish life is the outer natural life in man, which all animals also possess. <note>original: "ἄνθρωπος ψυχικὸς" (Greek: anthrōpos psychikos), referring to the "natural" or "soulish" man in 1 Corinthians 2:14.</note>
Regarding what the author understands by the **animalish spirit** in the work *Aurora*, see *Aurora* chapter 15, paragraph 35.
**Anti-Christ** [Anti-Christus], Antichrist, the Counter-Christ.
**Antimony** [Antimonium], antimony <note>original: "Spies-Glaß," an old term for stibnite or antimony ore</note>, it is a mineral.
**Anno**, in the year.
**Apocalypse** [Apocalypsis], the Revelation of Saint John.
**Apology** [Apologia], a defensive writing, a written accounting or justification to rescue one's honor and innocence.
**To Appeal** [Appelliren], to bring one's case before a higher court when one is not satisfied with the verdict of the lower courts.

<meta>catchword: Appen-</meta>

<summary>This page provides a glossary of terms from A to Ap, defining geographical, scientific, and theological concepts like Alchemy, Anatomy, and the "Animalish" life of man. It serves as a bridge between common Latinate vocabulary and Böhme's specific mystical usage.</summary>
<keywords>Africa, Alchemy, Alphabet, Alumnus, Anatomy, Animalia, Animalish life, Antichrist, Antimony, Apocalypse, Apology, Appeal</keywords>

--- Page 12 ---
<meta>This page continues the "First Index" or glossary of terms, moving from "Animalish" on the previous page to "Appendix" through "Aspect." It continues to define alchemical, theological, and philosophical terms within the worldview of Jacob Böhme and his contemporaries.</meta>

<page-num>11</page-num>
<header>Theosophical and Latin Words. A.</header>

**Appendix**, the supplement or addition.
**Approbation**, approval, or the applause given when one deems something good.
**Approve** [Approbiren], to favor, to deem good, to sanction.
**Application**, the appropriation or dedication <note>Often referring to how a spiritual truth is applied to the individual soul.</note>.
\* **Aquaster**, a heavenly essence or being formed from heavenly water, containing Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury <note>original: *Sal, Sulphur, Mercurius*. In Paracelsian alchemy, these "Three Principles" compose all matter.</note>.
**Arca, Archa**, the Ark, Noah’s chest.
**Arcanum**, a secret or hidden mystery.
\* **Archaeus** and **Separator**, in the mind, this is a fiery Mercury <note>original: *Mercurius*</note> with a dual effect: the **Archaeus** is the master craftsman and divider of the Essence <note>original: *Entis*</note> out of the Great Mystery <note>original: *Mysterio magno*</note>; the **Separator**, however, divides good and evil, light and dark being from one another, each into its own Principle <note>original: *Principium*</note>.
**Archia**, a principality or high sovereignty.
**Argumentum**, the argument, proof, or basis of a speech.
**Aristotle** [Aristoteles], a pagan world-sage or philosopher in Greece, at the time of Alexander the Great, before the birth of Christ; according to whose principles one usually teaches in schools, especially within the Papacy <note>The author reflects a typical 17th-century Protestant view, skeptical of Aristotle's influence on Catholic scholasticism.</note>.
**Aristotelian**, one who is devoted to his [Aristotle's] opinion.
**Arius**, an erroneous teacher in the 4th century <note>original: *seculo*</note> after Christ’s birth, who denied the eternal birth of the Son of God in the Person of Christ.
**Arian**, one who adheres to such an error.
**Article** [Articulus], a member; also a part of Christian doctrine as it is treated in spiritual or theological books.
**Artist** [Artista], an experienced chemist who possesses the Tincture <note>The "Tincture" was the elusive alchemical substance capable of transmutation.</note>, or the secret of making gold; an adept <note>original: *Adeptus*</note>.
\* **Ascendant**, an ascending star or constellation. (This is also applied spiritually to the mind.)
**Ascension**, the rising up or the going up into heaven.
**Asia**, the second part of the inhabited globe, containing Turkey and the other Eastern lands <note>original: *Morgen-Länder*, literally "morning-lands."</note>.
**Asmodus**, an impure, unchaste spirit; a spirit of frenzy and madness.
**Aspect** [Aspectus], the relative position of the planets when they stand in a certain proportion in the sky—beside, against, or under one another; this is also applied spiritually to the mind.

<meta>catchword: Astra-</meta>

<summary>This page provides a glossary of terms starting with 'A', blending traditional definitions with theosophical and alchemical interpretations of concepts like the Archaeus and Aquaster. It also includes brief historical-religious entries on Aristotle and the heretic Arius.</summary>

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>This page continues the "First Index" (Erstes Register) of terms, transitioning from the end of the letter "A" into the beginning of the letter "B." It defines astronomical, academic, and biblical terms within the context of 17th-century theosophy.</meta>

<page-num>12</page-num>
<header>A. B. First Index of the</header>

**Astral**, that which is of the nature of the stars and is driven by their power.
**Astrum** <note>original Latin: *Astrum*</note>, a constellation, the starry sky, or the firmament.
**Astrology** <note>original Latin: *Astrologia*</note>, the art of interpreting the stars, or the art of fortune-telling from the position of the constellations.
**Astrologer** <note>original Latin: *Astrologus*</note>, a star-interpreter who recognizes the influence of the firmament from the position of the stars and predicts things in advance.
**Astronomy** <note>original Latin: *Astronomia*</note>, star-lore, the knowledge of the starry sky.
**Astronomer** <note>original Latin: *Astronomus*</note>, a star-knower or star-seer who calculates and measures the outer course, the size, and the distance of the stars. By the Author, **Astrologer** and **Astronomer** are taken as one and the same, namely, as those learned in star-lore.
**Atheists** <note>original Latin: *Athei*</note>, those who believe in no God; also those who know nothing of God—animalistic, beast-like people.
**Golden Age** <note>original Latin: *Aureum seculum*</note>, the golden time.
**Aurora** <note>original Latin: *Aurora*</note>, the morning-redness <note>This is the title of Jacob Böhme’s first major work, symbolizing the breaking of spiritual light through the darkness of ignorance.</note>.
**Autograph** <note>original Latin: *Autographum*</note>, the handwritten script of an author, the original of a book.
**Author**, the originator, the actual writer and master of a book or another matter.
**Authority**, prestige or standing.
**Ave**, Good luck! Be greeted!

---

->B<-

**Baal**, thus the Canaanites named their idol; it actually means **Lord**.
**Babel**, Babylon, Confusion <note>A reference to the biblical Tower of Babel</note>; a city in Chaldea, belonging to the Turks <note>In the 17th century, the Ottoman Empire controlled the region of Mesopotamia/Iraq where ancient Babylon stood.</note>. Spiritually, it commonly signifies confused Christendom.
**Bachelor** <note>original: *Baccalaureus*</note>, the lowest academic title of honor for scholars in Philosophy, Medicine, and Theology; in some places, the lowest school colleagues are also called this.
**Bacchus**, the pagans' god of wine or drunkenness; also refers to gluttony.
**Basilisk** <note>original: *Basiliscus*</note>, a type of the most poisonous snake, which is said to poison and kill even with its gaze.

**Beel-zebub**, was the name of a Canaanite idol; it means a lord of vermin <note>literally "Lord of the Flies"</note>, the prince of devils; also a spirit of idolatry and false formation <note>original German: *Bildung*</note>.

**Carmen** <note>original Latin: *Carmen*</note>, a poem or a writing in verse; a song.

**Celebrate**, to observe solemnly or to feast.
**Central**, thorough, situated in or coming from the depths; original or foundational.

**Centrum Naturæ** <note>original Latin: *Centrum Naturæ*</note>, the innermost part of nature, the fierce sharpness of the nature-fire.
**Centriren**, to go to the ground or foundation of a thing.
**Ceremonies**, various types of church pageantry, displays of honor, or long-winded formalities.
**Cerinthus**, a teacher in the early Church who is said to have denied the divinity of Christ during the time of St. John.

Will to the eternal nature; the eye of the ether <note>original: *æthere*</note> in which everything lies, whatever time and eternity consist of and contain within themselves: And it is God Himself, as the [source] of all beings.
**Character**, characters, identifying marks, distinguishing signs, certain figures that indicate something specific, as used in Chemistry <note>original: *C. Medicin*, likely referring to Chymical Medicine or Paracelsianism</note>, Medicine, and in calendars; letters.
<margin>*</margin>**Cherub**, Cherubim, is an angel, [depicted] below with animal or calf-feet; thus animal below, angel above: [representing] a strict separation in its birth <note>likely meaning the internal spiritual development</note> until the angel rises out of the animal, which is free from all earthliness. (We humans have [this] birth within us.)
**Choleric**, of a fiery nature, rich in bile.
**Chorus**, a choir or a row of those singing praises to God, or of souls.
**Chymia**, the science of separating the pure and subtle in natural bodies from the coarse and impure, specifically through fire, for the preparation of medicines or other useful things; the Art of Separation <note>The 17th-century term for chemistry and alchemy</note>.
**Chymicus**, such an artist <note>a chemist or alchemist</note>.
**Circulus**, a circle, a round ring, the concept of roundness.
**Circumference**, the outermost perimeter of a thing, the concept of a circular rounding.
**Citation**, a summons before a court.
**Citatio prima**, the first legal summons.
**Cite**, to summon before a court; also to quote an author.
**Clavis**, the key <note>original Latin: *Clavis*</note>.

**Coaguliren**, to coagulate, to curdle, to become thick.
**Colloquium viatorum**, a conversation among pilgrims or wandering folk <note>original Latin: *Colloquium viatorum*</note>.
**Comœdia**, a joyful play or spectacle, a comedy.
**Commentarius**, an interpretation or commentary.
**Communicate**, to share, to make common.
**Compacta**, dense, hard things.
**Compactio**, the hardening or becoming-solid of a thing.

# Theosophical and Latin Words. C. 15

**To Compact**, to make hard and firm, to press together. <note>original: *Compactiren*</note>
**Complexion** <note>original: *Complexio*</note>, the natural, innate quality of humans, the temperament. There are primarily 4 of these: 1. **Choleric**, which is hot and dry; 2. **Melancholic**, which is cold and dry; 3. **Sanguine**, which is warm and moist; and 4. **Phlegmatic**, which is cold and moist. <note>This refers to the "Four Humors" theory of ancient medicine, where health and personality were determined by the balance of bodily fluids.</note>
**Complexioned**, naturally inclined to one or another quality.
**To Compose**, to join together, to manufacture. <note>original: *Componiren*</note>
**Council** <note>original: *Concilium, concilia*</note>, an assembly regarding matters of doctrine and faith.
**Concordance**, unity, agreement, harmony.
**To Accord**, to be in agreement, to harmonize. <note>original: *Concordiren*</note>
**To Condemn**, to judge, to sentence.
**To Confer**, to hold things together for comparison, to deliberate, to compare.
**Confession** <note>original: *Confessio*</note>, an acknowledgment, a confession of faith.
**Confirmation**, the validation, the strengthening.
**To Confirm**, to validate, to strengthen, to affirm.
**Confusion**, disorder, muddle.
**Conjunction** <note>original: *Conjunctio*</note>, the meeting, the union.
**Conspiracy** <note>original: *Conjuratio, conjurationes*</note>, secret plots, factions, mutinies.
**Assistant Headmaster** <note>original: *Conrector*</note>, the school official ranked immediately after the Headmaster <note>original: *Rector*</note>.
**Counsels** <note>original: *Consilia*</note>, resolutions, good advice.
**Constellation**, a certain position of the stars, especially the planets, when they stand either beside, opposite, or at a measured distance from one another, according to which their rays differ in effect. <note>In theosophy, stars were often seen as transmitters of divine or natural forces.</note>
**Constellated**, being of a nature shaped by the position of the stars.
**Constitution** <note>original: *Constitutio*</note>, the state, the quality or condition.
**It is finished** <note>original: *Consummatum est*</note>, it is completed, perfected. <note>This Latin phrase refers to the final words of Jesus on the cross (John 19:30).</note>
**To Continue**, to proceed.
**Contrary**, opposed to.
**Contrariety**, adversity.
**Opposite** <note>original: *Contrarium*</note>, a contrary thing, the opposite side.
**Conversation**, social interaction, company.
**To Converse**, to interact with one another, to keep company.
**Copy**, a transcript.
**To Couple**, to join together, to unite. <note>original: *Copuliren*</note>

## D

**Given** <note>original: *Datum*</note>, written or issued.
**Of** <note>original: *De*</note>, concerning or about.
**On Equanimity** <note>original: *De æquanimitate*</note>, concerning the state of being yielded or calm. <note>This refers to Böhme's concept of "Gelassenheit," or the resignation of the self-will to God.</note>
**On the Election of Grace** <note>original: *De electione gratiæ*</note>, concerning the choice of God's grace.
**On the Incarnation of the Word** <note>original: *De incarnatione verbi*</note>, concerning the Word becoming flesh.
**On True Repentance** <note>original: *De pœnitentia vera*</note>, concerning sincere penance.
**On the Four Temperaments** <note>original: *De quatuor complexionibus*</note>, concerning the four humors or natural qualities of man.
**On Regeneration** <note>original: *De regeneratione*</note>, concerning the New Birth.
**On the Signature of All Things** <note>original: *De signatura rerum*</note>, concerning the marking of things; how the inner life shines forth from the formation and outward shape of things. <note>A central theosophical concept: the belief that every object in nature is a "sign" or "signature" of its hidden spiritual essence.</note>
**On the Testaments of Christ** <note>original: *De testamentis Christi*</note>, concerning Christ’s holy sacraments.
**On the Three Principles** <note>original: *De tribus Principiis*</note>, concerning the three beginnings, births, or worlds. <note>These "Principles" represent the dark world of fire, the light world of love, and the visible world of nature.</note>
**On the Threefold Life** <note>original: *De triplici vita*</note>, concerning the triple life [of man].
**On the Mental Life** <note>original: *De vita mentali*</note>, concerning the supersensual or spiritual life of the soul.
**Decree** <note>original: *Decretum*</note>, a pronouncement, counsel, or ordinance.
**Dedication** <note>original: *Dedicatio*</note>, a dedicatory writing.
**Delete**, erase or wipe out. <note>original: *Dele*</note>
**To Distill**, to drive moisture out of a thing in the form of vapor so that it collects in drops and can thus be skillfully captured.

# Theosophical and Latin Words. D. E.

**To Dictate** <note>original: *Dictiren*</note>, to say something to another that they should write down.
**Day** <note>original: *Dies* (Latin)</note>.
**Dignity** <note>original: *Dignitas*</note>, highness, or majesty.
* **Dialectics** <note>original: *Dialectica*</note>, a reasoning-art, where one seeks to investigate the *Why*, the cause, and the property of all things through the senses. It is commonly called "reasoning" <note>original: *raisonniren* (French/German)</note>, which involves certain clever tricks. **Dialectical**: that which is handled in this manner.
**Disciple** <note>original: *Discipulus*</note>, a student or apprentice.
**To Dispute** <note>original: *Disputiren*</note>, to argue or fight over an opinion.
**Disputant** <note>original: *Disputirer*</note>, one who quarrels over opinions.
**Disputable** <note>original: *Disputirlich*</note>, doubtful; what is not yet settled or remains a matter of contention.
**Doctor**, a teacher; it is the highest title of honor for scholars at universities <note>original: *hohen Schulen* (high schools/universities)</note>.
**To Double** <note>original: *Dupliciren*</note>.

->E<-

**Ecstasy** <note>original: *Eckstasis* (from Greek *ekstasis*)</note>, a state of rapture or being carried away.
* **Eden**, a certain place where Paradise opened up, and eternity bloomed within time; where heaven holds still through the earth (which stands desolate there), and God keeps still and lets Himself work through it. <note>In Böhme’s thought, Eden is the point where the eternal spiritual world intersects with the temporal physical world.</note>
**Edition**, the preparation or issuing of a book for printing.
**Effect** <note>original: *Effectus*</note>, the impact or result.
**To Effectuate**, to put to work, to accomplish.
**Excretion** <note>original: *Egestus* (Latin)</note>, that which is foamed out, the froth, or the byproduct of fermentation <note>original: *der Jaͤscht, die Ausjaͤhrung*</note>.
**God** <note>original: *El* (Hebrew)</note>.
**Election** <note>original: *Electio*</note>, the choice.
* **Element** <note>original: *Elementum*</note>, **Elements** are the bodies of the properties of nature, in which nature becomes corporeal. The **Eternal Element** is the essence of Divine corporeality, and also the body of our souls in Christ. <note>Böhme differentiates between the four volatile earthly elements and the single, "Pure Element" of the spiritual world.</note>
**Elemental** or **Elementish**, that which consists of such natural bodies.
**My God** <note>original: *Eli* (Hebrew)</note>.
**Emmanuel**, or **Immanu-El**, God with us.
* **Being** <note>original: *Ens* (Latin)</note>, the most hidden part of a seed, or something that is the blooming of an essence. <note>In alchemy and theosophy, the *Ens* is the vital, spiritual core of a substance.</note>
**Essential** <note>original: *Entalisch*</note>, having the nature of an essence or being.

It begins mid-sentence, concluding the definition for "Exodus" from the previous page, and covers terms from "Experience" through "Formally." <header>Theosophical and Latin Words. E. F.</header>
<sig>b 2</sig>

...[Exo]dus is called so, because it deals with the departure of Israel from Egypt. <note>The text begins mid-word, completing the entry for "Exodus."</note>

\* **Experience** <note>original: *Experientia*</note>, trial, discovery, or finding out. Inward experience occurs when a person is well-tested, having passed through all kinds of trials and spiritual struggles, and possesses practiced inward senses.

**Explanation** <note>original: *Explicatio*</note>, the interpretation or declaration.

**Explanation of terms** <note>original: *Explicatio terminorum*</note>, the explanation of unknown words and ways of speaking.

**Extract** <note>original: *Extract*</note>, an excerpt from a book; also taken from other things.

**By descent** <note>original: *Extraduce*</note>, through propagation or the generation of the seed. <note>This refers to Traducianism, the belief that the soul is transmitted from parents to children through biological birth, rather than being newly created by God for each person.</note>

### F

**Fable** <note>original: *Fabula*</note>, a fictional story, a little tale.

**Result** <note>original: *Facit*</note>, "it makes"; also, what comes out in a calculation—the sum that has been sought and found through reckoning.

**Let there be** <note>original: *Fiat*</note>, "it shall be"; the word used at the Creation.

**Figure** <note>original: *Figura*</note>, the shape or the form.

**To figure** <note>original: *Figuriren*</note>, to form or to give a shape.

**Figuring** <note>original: *Figurirung*</note>, the formation.

**Finance** <note>original: *Finanz*</note>, a cunning plot to obtain money.

**Firmament** <note>original: *Firmamentum*</note>, the vault of heaven, the starry sky, the firmament.

\* **Fixed** <note>original: *Fixus*</note>, firm, steadfast. That which is "fixed" in man is God himself, and our inward, heavenly body derived from Christ. <note>In Böhme's alchemy-influenced theology, the "fixed" is the eternal, unchanging element of the soul that survives the "fire" of divine judgment.</note>

**Flacian** <note>original: *Flacianer*</note>, a follower of the opinions of Flacius. Matthias Flacius was a Lutheran teacher and for some time a professor at the universities of Wittenberg and Jena. He taught, among other things, that original sin itself is the substance or essence of man. Because of this teaching, he was removed from his office and harshly persecuted until he died in exile in 1575.

**Flower** <note>original: *Flos*</note>, the blossom or bloom.

**Folio** <note>original: *Folio*</note>, on the leaf or page.

**Source** <note>original: *Fons*</note>, a spring or well.

**Form** <note>original: *Forma*</note>, the shape, outline, or pattern. **In form** <note>original: *In forma*</note>, in the shape or manner of.

**Formally** <note>original: *Formaliter*</note>, in an orderly fashion, as is proper.

The definitions continue to blend linguistic, theological, and theosophical concepts common in 17th and 18th-century spiritual literature.</meta>

<page-num>20</page-num>
<header>F. G. H. First Index of the</header>

**To form** <note>original: *Formen, formiren*</note>, to mold, to build, to give a shape.
**Former**, a shaper, a Creator.
**Formation** <note>original: *Formirung, Formung*</note>, the building or shaping.
**Formula**, a prescribed way and manner, a rule or instruction.
**Foundation** <note>original: *Fundamentum*</note>, the ground of a thing upon which it stands. <note>The OCR ends with "beste," likely "besteht" (stands/exists).</note>

## G

**Gabriel** <note>original: *Gabri-El*</note>, a man of **GOD**, is the name of an angel who was also sent to the Virgin Mary.
**Gehenna**, hell.
**Gems** <note>original: *Gemma, Gemmen*</note>, precious stones.
**Genesis**, birth, the Creation. This is the name of the First Book of Moses, because it begins with the Creation.
**General Title**, the main name or main title of a book.
**Generation** <note>original: *Generatio*</note>, the procreation, birth.
\* **Genius**, a birth-angel <note>an attendant spirit assigned to a person at birth</note>, is of three kinds: 1) The Genius in the Light, which drives us toward all that is good. 2) The natural Genius in the Fire, which gives a person wit and understanding, with which one governs the outward life. 3) The dark Genius is the *Evectrum* <note>original: *Evektrum*; likely referring to a spectral double or a shadow-spirit</note>.
**Globe** <note>original: *Globus, globul*</note>, the sphere or ball.
**Glory** <note>original: *Gloria*</note>, the honor, splendor.
**Glorification**, the making glorious; **glorified**, made glorious.
**Gloss** <note>original: *Glossa*</note>, a note next to the text, an interpretation.
**Know thyself** <note>original Greek: *Gnothi se auton*</note>.
**Degree** <note>original: *Gradus*</note>, a step, a rung, a stride, a certain measure, or an elevation; **by degrees** <note>original: *gradibus*</note>, according to the steps.
**To graduate** <note>original: *Gradiren*</note>, to elevate, to bring to a measured purpose.
**Grammar** <note>original: *Grammatica*</note>, the art of language, is a treatise on the different kinds of words and their proper use.
**Governor** <note>original: *Gubernator*</note>, a regent, a representative or governor.

## H

\* **Habit** <note>original: *Habitus*</note>, clothing; it is also a state of the mind, where one has grasped a thing well within oneself, a matter that one understands well.
**Thus far he** <note>original Latin: *Hactenus ille*</note>, "so far, up to here that one," namely, the quoted words. <note>A scholarly shorthand indicating the end of a quotation.</note>
**Hallelujah**, praise the **LORD**.
**Harmony** <note>original: *Harmonia*</note>, the agreement, of one mind and spirit.

<page-num>21</page-num>
<header>Theosophical and Latin Words. H. J. 21</header>

**To harmonize**, to agree, to be of one mind.
**Hercules**, a pagan hero who, in their opinion, was deified after he endured many great and difficult labors and overcame gruesome monsters.
**Hesperides**, the name given in pagan fables to three sisters born of Night <note>original Latin: *Nocte*</note> alone, who possessed a garden with golden apples beyond the great world-ocean; it was guarded by a dragon so that no one could obtain them until Hercules slew it and carried the apples away.
**Hierarch** <note>original: *Hierarcha*</note>, a sacred head, a prince of the throne.
**Hierarchy** <note>original: *Hierarchia*</note>, a sacred or spiritual princedom and government.
**History** <note>original: *Historia*</note>, the story.
**Historian** <note>original: *Historicus*</note>, a writer of history.
**To court** <note>original: *Hofiren*</note>, to flatter, to caress, after the manner of the court.
**Man** <note>original: *Homo*</note>, the human being; **of man** <note>original: *hominis*</note>.
**Host** <note>original: *Hostia*</note>, a sacrifice.

## I

**Jachi-El**, the Lord of the Living, my God; it is the name of a princely angel.
**Jah**, the hidden God.
**Ibidem**, in the very same place.
**Idea**, an image, a draft, a conceived notion of a thing.
**Ideal**, figuratively or in the form of an image.
**Idiot** <note>original: *Idiota*; in this historical context, it refers to an unlearned person or a layman, rather than its modern derogatory meaning</note>.
**Jehoshua**, the name of God from the ground of Light: Jesus or Joshua.
**Jehovah**, the name of God from the ground of Fire, which the Jews are not permitted to pronounce.
* **Iliaster**, *iliastrum*, is the mystery <note>original: *Mysterium*</note> of the divine being, outside of and prior to nature <note>This is a term used by Paracelsus to describe the primeval matter or the foundation of the cosmos</note>.
* **Iliastric**, that which has its origin in eternal divine freedom and is reborn in Christ.
* **Imagination** <note>original: *Imaginatio*</note>, a power of the mind <note>original: *Gemüth*</note> in nature and spirit.
* **Imprints** <note>original: *Impressen*</note>, that which is drawn into the will; it happens through the dark desire of nature.
**Impression** <note>original: *Impressio*</note>, a hard, strict power of imagination.
**To imprint** <note>original: *Imprimiren*</note>, to press in, to firmly imagine.

<header>I. First Index of the</header>

*Incantation* <note>original: *Incantatio*</note>, the adjuration, enchantment, sorcery.
*Incarnation* <note>original: *Incarnatio*</note>, the becoming human.
*Inclination*, *Inclining*, affection, benevolence.
*To incorporate*, to embody.
*Index*, a register, a guide.
*Indifferent*, impartial, neutral.
*Hell* <note>original: *Infernum*</note>, the lowest place on earth, the dwelling of damned souls.
*To infect* <note>original: *Inficiren*</note>, to contaminate, to poison.
*Infection* <note>original: *Inficirung*</note>, contagion.
*To inflame* <note>original: *Inflammiren*</note>, to ignite, to heat.
*Influence*, *influences*, the effect of the stars upon physical bodies.
*To influence* <note>original: *Influiren*</note>, to flow in, to act upon.
*Instruction on the Last Things* <note>original: *Informatorium Novissimorum*</note>, teaching regarding the final things or times.
*Infula*, a bishop’s miter or hat.
*Ingenium*, the power of reason to awaken thoughts or mental images.
*Ingenious* <note>original: *Ingeniosus*</note>, clever, rich in ideas; one who can awaken pleasant and graceful thoughts and images within themselves.
*In potential* <note>original: *In potentia*</note>, in capacity, in power.
\* *To in-qualify* <note>original: *Inqualiren*; a technical term in the theosophy of Jacob Böhme describing how different qualities or powers work together</note>, this is best understood in faith, when the will of the soul in the Light "in-qualifies" with the love of God and becomes one will and essence with God; likewise *In-qualifying*.
*In particular* <note>original: *In specie*</note>, especially.
*Inspiration*, the breathing-in of the Spirit.
*Instrument*, *instruments* <note>original: *Instrumentum*, *instrumenta*</note>, tools, a musical performance.
*Island* <note>original: *Insula*</note>, a piece of land entirely surrounded by water.
*In summary* <note>original: *In summa*</note>, overall, in short, in a word.
*Intellect* <note>original: *Intellectus*</note>, the understanding, comprehension.
*To intend* <note>original: *Intendiren*</note>, to purpose, to aim for, to eye.
*Intention*, the purpose, the aim, the focal point.
*Intercession*, the petition, advocacy.
*In the great confusion* <note>original: *In turba magna*</note>. The author uses this to describe the awakened wrath within nature <note>in Böhmean thought, the "Turba" is the disruption of natural harmony by the fire-principle or wrath</note>.
*Jovial*, according to the nature and impulse of the planet Jupiter.
*Jovialist*, one who is of the nature of Jupiter.

<header>Theosophical and Latin **Words.** I. K. L.</header>

Likewise <note>original: *Item*</note>, the same.

Jubilee Year <note>original: *Jubel-Jahr*</note>, a year of remission, of freedom and joys.

To jubilate <note>original: *Jubiliren*</note>, to shout for joy, to exult.

To judge <note>original: *Judiciren*</note>, to rule, to pass judgment, to state one's opinion on something.

Judgment <note>original: *Judicium*</note> is the power of reason to examine awakened thoughts and images, and to distinguish those that are real and correct from those that are empty and unfounded.

Julian <note>original: *Julianus*</note>, a Roman Emperor in the 4th century <note>original: *seculo*</note> after the birth of Christ; he fell away from the Christian religion to paganism and was therefore called the Apostate <note>original: *Apostata*</note>, or the renegade.

*Jupiter. This property is taken as the second form <note>Jacob Böhme's "Seven Properties" or "Forms" (*Gestalten*) describe the stages of divine manifestation</note> of eternal and temporal nature, being the free desire of eternity impressed by Saturn, from which Jove <note>Latin: *Jovi*; a poetic name for Jupiter</note> receives corporeality. In the heavens, it is a planetary star so named, of a benevolent nature. In the elemental-astral human, it governs the brain in the head; among the metals, it represents tin, and its symbol is ♃.*

Laws <note>original: *Jura*</note>, the rights, statutes.

Jurists <note>original: *Juristen*</note>, those who have studied the law so that they may know and advise what is in accordance with it or not.

### K

Kabbalah <note>original: *Kabala*</note>, see *Cabala*.

### L

Laborer <note>original: *Laborant*</note>, a worker; the term is mostly used for chemists <note>In this period, "chemistry" or "chymistry" often referred to alchemy</note> who work with fire, either to prepare medicines or other artistic things, and especially to discover the philosophers' stone <note>original: *lapidem Philos.*</note>.

Laboratory <note>original: *Laboratorium*</note>, a chemical <note>alchemical</note> workshop.

Stone <note>original: *Lapis*</note>, a stone.

The Philosophers' Stone <note>original: *Lapis Philosophorum*</note>, the "Stone of the Wise"; this is what the secret art is called, which transforms base metals into the purest gold through a dry or wet tincture <note>An alchemical substance believed to have the power to "tinge" or transform matter</note>; such a tincture is also said to be an infallible remedy against all diseases.

The Cornerstone of the Wise <note>original: *Lapis Philosophorum angularis*</note>, Christ.

<header>L. First Index of the</header>

**Larva**, the mask, disguise, an imitation face, masque.

**Legatus** <note>original: Latin *Legatus*</note>, an envoy, ambassador.

**Legio** <note>original: Latin *Legio*</note>, Legion, a troop of Roman soldiers, commonly numbering 6,666 men; in general, every host or heavenly army was named thus.

**Leviticus**, Levit. This is the name of the 3rd book of Moses, which deals with the laws of the tribe of Levi, or the Jewish **Divine**-Service.

**Lex** <note>original: Latin *Lex, leges*</note>, the law, the laws.

**Liber** <note>original: Latin *Liber*</note>, a book.

**Libellus Apologeticus** <note>original: Latin</note>, a defense or protective writing <note>An "apology" in the classical sense: a formal justification or defense of a doctrine</note>.

***Limbus**, Spiritually, it is the heavenly Fire-World of the Father's eternal nature, the spiritual heaven and earth in which **God** the Father begets his Son—the eternal, constant Word—in our souls. It is the Principle in which the Son dwells in the Father. According to nature, it is the soul of the spirit of this world in man; which, after the rebirth from Christ and spiritual illumination in the essence, according to both tinctures of fire and light, is replenished and becomes a Male Virgin <note>In Böhmean theosophy, the "Male Virgin" or "Androgyne" represents the restored, perfected state of humanity, reuniting the masculine and feminine spiritual principles lost at the Fall</note>.

***Limus**, is the purest essence of the earth, the extract of the same, according to each Principle. Mud of the earth <note>original: *Limus terræ*</note> is the same thing.

**Liquor**, a fluid matter.

**Linea**, line, a stroke, row, divisions of lineages or families, kinship.

**Locality** <note>original: *Localitær*</note>, spatiality, containment.

**Loci communes** <note>original: Latin, meaning "commonplaces"</note>, are all kinds of words kept according to the alphabet, under which one is accustomed to write down occurring matters in detail (each under its appropriate word, as if under its own title), so that upon looking up such a word, one may simultaneously remember the most noteworthy things associated with it. See, for example, the 3rd Index.

**Loco solis** <note>original: Latin</note>, in the sun's place or stead.

**Locus** <note>original: Latin</note>, a place, position.

<header>Theosophical and Latin Words. L. M.</header>

**Logica** <note>original: Latin *Logica*</note>, Logic, is a science of forming one's thoughts correctly and clearly and proving them convincingly.
**Logical**, what pertains to or follows according to Logic.
**Longinus**, the name given to the one who is said to have pierced Christ's side with a spear while He was on the cross.
**Lucerna** <note>original: Latin</note>, a light or lantern.
**Lucifer**, the name of the Devil, a Prince of Thrones, so called because of his former splendor of light before the Fall; the Morning Star is also called by this name, as is the planet Venus when it appears in the morning.
***Luna**, the Moon, the 7th property of both natures. <note>In theosophy, "both natures" usually refers to the eternal/divine nature and the temporal/external nature</note>. Saturn creates the image-forming quality within her; her metal is Silver, and her sign is ☽.
**Lunar**, what is of this quality or of the nature of the Moon.

## M

**Macrocosmus** <note>original: Latin, "the great world"</note>, the visible great world, such as the starry heavens together with the Earth.
**Macula** <note>original: Latin</note>, a spot or mark.
***Magia divina** <note>original: Latin, "Divine Magic"</note>, is the weaving of the subtle spirit of the will within the growth of the Principles, where the will forms nothing itself; rather, it manifests itself with the fiery desire of love through the heart of **God**, where the Holy Spirit is the shaper and former of the powers within the heavenly **tincture** <term>Tincture: A spiritual or alchemical essence that infuses, colors, and transforms reality; here it refers to the spiritual quality of fire and light</term> of fire and light, which lies in the unity within Christ’s humanity—our divine Ark of the Covenant—over which the Holy Spirit hovers. However, the will provides the essence for the formation and takes its own being, such as the external creature or the beast which it carries upon itself, and casts it into the eternal Word, thereby losing its life of selfhood. Whereupon **God** creates something new, which faith takes and receives—that which it has asked for in the name of **Jesus** out of the fullness of divine grace. And the Holy Spirit gives Himself thereby into the will, bringing the divine gift, and is Himself the fulfiller within the creature.
***Magia naturalis** <note>original: Latin, "Natural Magic"</note>, the light of nature within the understanding.
***Magus** <note>original: Latin, "Mage" or "Magician"</note>, is a believer who walks in the spirit and deals with divine wisdom, enlightened spirit-

possesses [spiritual] senses and mind. **Magical**, what is worked through magic, and is understood according to its nature or manner.

**Matrix** <note>original: Latin *matrix naturae*</note>, the "womb of nature," the eternal mother of the birthing process; in the 1st Principle, it is the harshness/astringency; in the 2nd Principle, it is the gentle mother of the water-spirit.

### Theosophical and Latin Words. M. 27

**Mausim** or **Maußim**, a fortress, and an idol of this name. <note>Likely a reference to the "god of fortresses" mentioned in Daniel 11:38, often interpreted in the early modern period as a pagan deity or a symbol of the Antichrist.</note>

**Maximum**, the greatest.

**Medicine** <note>original: Latin *Medicamentum*</note>, remedy.

**Medicine** <note>original: Latin *Medicina*</note>, the medicine or the medical art.

**Doctor of Medicine** <note>original: Latin *Medicinæ Doctor*</note>, a learned physician; this is the most distinguished title of honor for physicians, which they receive from the university <note>original: *hohen Schule*</note>.

**Physician** <note>original: Latin *Medicus*</note>, a doctor who thoroughly understands the nature of the human body, its diseases, the remedies useful against them, and their proper use, and is able to provide counsel and help therein.

**Moderate-Fire** <note>original: *Median-Feuer*</note>, a medium or temperate fire.

**To Meditate**, to reflect, contemplate, or think through.

**Melancholia**, melancholy, gloominess, sadness.

**Melancholic**, gloomy; this is one of the four complexions <note>A reference to the "four humors" theory of personality: sanguine, choleric, melancholic, and phlegmatic</note> or temperaments of human beings.

**Memorial**, a monument, a memory book, or a reminder note.

\* **Mind** <note>original: Latin *Mens*</note>, the spirit or soul, is an image of divine revelation and a figure of the great name of GOD; a ray of His omnipotence; an instrument and workshop of the Holy Spirit; a desiring will for the re-revelation of divine power; a companion of divine wisdom and holiness; the wheel of the birth of life in the heavenly birth-mother; a school of combat for the Holy Spirit, wherein—in the will regained from darkness to light—faith, love, and hope arise, and the inward man is born.

**Solvent** <note>original: Latin *Menstruum*</note>, in chemistry <note>at this time, "Chymie" referred to both chemistry and alchemy</note> signifies a moisture which has the power to penetrate and dissolve things cast into it, and also to draw out their power and color and pull it into itself.

**Mental**, pertaining to the mind.

\* **Mercury** <note>original: Latin *Mercurius*</note>, is the sixth property or form of nature, from which the planetary star of this name was formed in the outer heavens. In the mind, after the darkness, it creates the deep torment of anxiety; after the light, it creates the <note>The text cuts off here; the catchword for the next page is "the" (das).</note>

# M. First Register of

...powerful benevolence. In the external world, this celestial body is located in the bladder. Among the metals, it is mercury <note>original: *Queck-ſilber*</note>. Its symbol is ☿.

**Mercurial**, what is of this nature; a **Mercurialist** is a person who stands under the influence or possesses the characteristics of Mercury.

**Mesh**, red earth, a mixture of fire and water. <note>original: *Meſch*. In theosophical contexts, this often refers to a primordial or alchemical substance related to the creation of the first human.</note>

**Messiah**, the Anointed One, Christ.

**Metal** <note>original: Latin *Metallum*</note>, metal, ore.

**Metallic**, that which is made of metal or ore.

**Micha-El**, "Who is like God?" The strength of God; this is the name of the first Archangel <note>original: *Thron-Engels*, literally "Throne-Angel"</note>, according to the attribute of the Father.

**Microcosm** <note>original: Latin *Microcosmus*</note>, the "little world," which refers to Man. <note>A central concept in Hermetic philosophy suggesting that the human being is a miniature reflection of the entire universe (the Macrocosm).</note>

**Millennium** <note>original: Latin *Millenarium*</note>, the thousand-year reign of Christ.

**Minerals** <note>original: Latin *Mineralia*</note>, all kinds of ores and "mountain-growths" <note>An early modern term for substances found in the earth, which were believed to "grow" in the soil like plants</note> that are brought forth from the earth.

**Lesser Minerals** <note>original: Latin *Mineralia minora*</note>, the lesser mountain-growths, such as sulfur, saltpeter, antimony <note>original: *Spies-Glaß*</note>, etc.

**Minister**, a servant or attendant.

**Ministry** <note>original: Latin *Ministerium*</note>, the service, the office, <term>also</term> <note>original: Latin *item*</note> the collective number of preachers in a particular place.

**Miracle** <note>original: Latin *Miraculum*</note>, a wondrous work.

**Miserere**, "have mercy." <note>A common liturgical expression, typically referring to the beginning of Psalm 51.</note>

**Model**, any pattern, the blueprint of a thing according to which it is made.

**Tinder** <note>original: *Moder*</note>, actually means any matter in which a small spark can easily catch, be held, and glow, and which can be turned into a burning fire by fanning or blowing; whether it be all sorts of kindling, especially from dry and decayed wood, or ash, wick, and the like. **Glowing tinder** is when a spark is actually present in the kindling or another flammable matter, but cannot yet reach a proper flame.

**Monarch** <note>original: Latin *Monarcha*</note>, a free regent, king, or emperor who rules alone with full power.

**Monarchy** <note>original: Latin *Monarchia*</note>, a free, unrestricted dominion.

**Monster** <note>original: Latin *Monstrum*</note>, a deformity, a monster, or a beast.

<meta>catchword: * Mon-</meta>

**Theosophical and Latin Words. M. N. 29**

**Monstrous** <note>original: *Monstrosisch*</note>, very deformed, hideous.
**Monument** <note>original: *Monumentum*</note>, a tomb, an honorable memorial of the deceased.
**Mordio**, a cry of "murder!" or a loud lament. <note>original: *Zeter*. An archaic German exclamation used to raise an alarm or express great distress.</note>
**Mors**, Death.
**Mortification**, the killing off [of the flesh], martyrdom. <note>In theosophical and alchemical contexts, this refers to the "death" of the ego or the breakdown of a substance's base form to allow for spiritual or physical transformation.</note>
**Motives** <note>original: *Motiven*</note>, causes that move us toward something.
**Motus**, movement, motion, or an uprising.
**Multiplication**, increase or augmentation.
**Multiply** <note>original: *Multipliciren*</note>, to increase.
**Mumia**, this is the name for the embalmed bodies of those who died in Egypt in ancient times, which are excavated still undecayed; some also use them as medicine. <note>In the early modern period, ground "mummy" was a common, albeit gruesome, ingredient in European pharmacies.</note>
**Musica**, the art of singing; also, the art of playing instruments.
**Mysterium**, a secret; *mysteria*, secrets.
**\*Mysterium Magnum**, the Great Mystery; the outflow and counter-reflection <note>original: *Gegenwurf*</note> of divine knowledge. <note>A central term in the philosophy of Jacob Böhme, referring to the primordial state or the "chaos" from which all creation is manifested.</note>
**Mystica**, hidden things, spiritual secret speeches or matters.

->N<-

**Natio**, a nation, a lineage, a people.
**Nativitas**, nativity, birth; also, a prophecy drawn from the hour of birth <note>Refers to an astrological birth chart or horoscope.</note>.
**Natura**, nature, the world of forces; also, the life within the elements.
**Naturalis**, natural.
**Necrolice**, the first three forms of the dark world. <note>Likely a specialized theosophical term referring to the initial, "contractive" stages of spiritual manifestation.</note>
**Necromantice**, according to the world of fire, or according to the properties of the fire-soul.
**Necromantia**, *nigromantia*, the black art, dark transformation, magic, or sorcery.
**Necromanticus**, *nigromanticus*, a practitioner of the black arts, a dark magus.
**Nobis**, to us.
**Nobis infernum**, "yes, hell for us," is a common proverb.
**Nota**, mark or take notice; also, a remark. **Nota bene**, **NB.**, note well.
**Num**, **Numeri**, the numbers; this is the name of the Fourth Book of Moses <note>The Book of Numbers in the Old Testament.</note>.

## M. First Index of the...

...powerful beneficence. In the outer [macrocosm], this celestial body is associated with the bladder. Among metals, it is quicksilver. The symbol is ☿.

**Mercurial**, whatever is of this kind; a "Mercurialist" is a person who possesses the characteristics of Mercury <note>In early modern psychology, this often meant someone quick-witted, volatile, or eloquent</note>.

**Mesch**, red earth; a mixture of fire and water <note>original: *Mesch*. This is a term used in theosophical alchemy, often referring to the "Adamite" earth or the primordial substance of man</note>.

**Messiah**, the Anointed One, Christ.

**Metal** <note>original: Latin *Metallum*</note>, ore.

**Metallic**, whatever is made of metal or ore.

**Michael** <note>original: *Micha-El*</note>, who is like GOD? The strength of God; this is the name of the first Archangel of the Throne, according to the Father’s property <note>In the system of Jakob Böhme and his followers, Michael represents the divine power of the Father-principle</note>.

**Microcosm** <note>original: Latin *Microcosmus*</note>, the little world, Man <note>A central philosophical idea that the human being is a miniature version of the entire universe</note>.

**Millennium** <note>original: Latin *Millenarium*</note>, the thousand-year kingdom of Christ.

**Minerals** <note>original: Latin *Mineralia*</note>, all kinds of ores and "mountain-growths" <note>original: *Berg-Gewächse*, an early term for minerals and crystals believed to "grow" in the earth like plants</note> brought forth from the ground.

**Lesser minerals** <note>original: Latin *Mineralia minora*</note>, the inferior mountain-growths, such as sulfur, saltpeter, antimony <note>original: *Spies-Glaß*</note>, etc.

**Minister**, a servant or attendant.

**Ministry** <note>original: Latin *Ministerium*</note>, the service, the office, etc.; also the collective body of preachers in a specific place.

**Miracle** <note>original: Latin *Miraculum*</note>, a wonder-work.

**Have mercy** <note>original: Latin *Miserere*</note>.

**Model**, any pattern, the draft or design of a thing according to which it is manufactured.

**Moder** <note>original: *Moder*</note>, tinder or decay; actually signifies any matter in which a small spark can easily catch, hold, smolder, and be fanned into a burning fire by blowing upon it; whether it be any kind of tinder, especially from dry and rotted wood, or ash, wick, and the like. "Smoldering moder" is when a spark is actually present in the tinder or another ignitable matter but cannot reach a proper flame.

**Monarch** <note>original: Latin *Monarcha*</note>, a free ruler, King, or Emperor, who alone governs with full and absolute power.

**Monarchy** <note>original: Latin *Monarchia*</note>, a free, unrestricted dominion.

**Monster** <note>original: Latin *Monstrum*</note>, a birth defect, a monstrosity, or a beast.

### Theosophical and Latin Words. M. N. 29

**Monstrous**, very misshapen, abominable.

**Monument** <note>original: Latin *Monumentum*</note>, a tomb, a memorial in honor of the deceased.

**Murder!** <note>original: *Mordio*</note>, a cry of "Murder!" or a loud scream for help.

**Death** <note>original: Latin *Mors*</note>.

**Mortification**, the killing off <note>This usually refers to the "killing" of the earthly desires or the "old man" in a spiritual sense</note>, torment.

**Motives**, causes that move us toward something.

**Movement** <note>original: Latin *Motus*</note>, motion, or an uprising/commotion.

**Multiplication**, the increase or reproduction.

**To multiply**, to increase.

**Mummy** <note>original: *Mumia*</note>, this is the name given to the bodies of those who died in ancient times in Egypt and were embalmed, which are still dug up today in an undecayed state; they are also used by some as medicine <note>In the 17th century, "mumia" (powdered remains) was a common, if controversial, ingredient in the pharmacopeia</note>.

**Music** <note>original: Latin *Musica*</note>, the art of singing; also the art of playing instruments.

**Mystery** <note>original: Latin *Mysterium*</note>, a secret; *mysteria*: the secrets.

***Mysterium Magnum*** <note>Latin: "The Great Mystery"</note>, the Great Mystery; the outflow and reflection <note>original: *Gegenwurf*, literally "counter-throw." In Böhme’s thought, this is the manifestation or "mirroring" of the divine will into a comprehensible form</note> of divine knowledge.

**Mystical** <note>original: Latin *Mystica*</note>, hidden things; spiritual, hidden speeches or matters.

->N<-

**Nation** <note>original: Latin *Natio*</note>, a race, a people.

**Nativity** <note>original: Latin *Nativitas*</note>, birth; also a prophecy or horoscope derived from the hour of birth.

**Nature** <note>original: Latin *Natura*</note>, the world of forces; also the life within the elements.

**Natural** <note>original: Latin *Naturalis*</note>.

**Necrolice**, the first three forms of the dark world <note>A term specific to Böhmean theosophy, describing the harsh, "death-like" qualities of the first three properties of nature (attraction, friction, and anguish) before they are ignited by the light</note>.

**Necromantically** <note>original: *Necromantice*</note>, according to the fire-world, or according to the quality of the fire-souls.

**Necromancy** <note>original: *Necromantia, nigromantia*</note>, the "black art," dark transformation, magic, sorcery.

**Necromancer** <note>original: *Necromanticus, Nigromanticus*</note>, a practitioner of the black arts, a dark magus.

**To us** <note>original: Latin *Nobis*</note>.

**Hell for us** <note>original: *Nobis infernum*</note>, "even hell," which is a common proverb.

**Note** <note>original: Latin *Nota*</note>, mark; also a remark. ***Nota bene*** (NB): mark well, pay close attention.

**Numbers** <note>original: Latin *Num. Numeri*</note>, the numbers; this is also the name of the Fourth Book of Moses.

O. P. First Index of the... <note>This header is likely a continuation of "First Index of Theosophical and Latin Words."</note>

...because it begins with the counting of the Israelite tribes. <note>The entry for the Book of Numbers, continued from the previous page.</note>
**Number** <note>original: *Numerus*</note>, the count.
**Nourishment** <note>original: *Nutrimentum*</note>, food or sustenance.

### O

**Object** <note>original: *Objectum*</note>, what one has at hand, what one deals with, a subject or a counter-subject. <note>original: *Vorwurf, Gegenwurf*. In theosophical writing, these terms refer to the object of perception or the reflection of a divine idea.</note>
**Objection**, a counter-argument in a conversation.
**The Occident** <note>original: *Occidens*</note>, the setting of the sun, toward evening, the West.
**Economy** <note>original: *Oeconomia*</note>, the management of a household.
**Oedipus**, signifies a witty, sharp-minded man who can quickly perceive and guess at a matter. <note>A reference to the Greek mythological figure who solved the riddle of the Sphinx.</note>
**Oil** <note>original: *Oleum*</note>, the Oil, the Quintessence. <note>In alchemy, the quintessence is the purest, most concentrated essence extracted from a substance.</note>
**Oleaginous** <note>original: *Olitætisch*</note>, oily, or of an oily nature.
**Onyx** <note>original: *Onix*</note>, a white, blackish, and bluish translucent gemstone.
**Operate** <note>original: *Operiren*</note>, to work or act.
**Opinion**, a conjecture, a viewpoint, or a notion.
**Oracle** <note>original: *Oraculum*</note>, a divine voice, answer, or prophecy; the audible answer of pagan idols when they were asked for advice.
**The Orient** <note>original: *Oriens*</note>, the rising of the sun, toward morning, the East.
**Original**, a master’s own work, writing, or painting.
**Oval**, egg-shaped, an elongated round.

### P

**Page** <note>original: *Pag., Pagina*</note>, the leaf; on this or that page.
**Pansophic** <note>original: *Pansophicum*</note>, that which deals with all sciences; also, with the main points of revealed wisdom. <note>Pansophism was a 17th-century intellectual movement aiming to organize all human knowledge into a single, divine system.</note>
**Parable**, a representation through a comparison or likeness.
**Parabolic** <note>original: *Parabolisch*</note>, by way of a likeness, metaphorical.
**Paracelsus**, Theophrastus Paracelsus, a very famous physician and philosopher in the time of Luther, who primarily practiced and popularized chemistry. He died in Salzburg in 1541.
**Paracelsian** <note>original: *Paracelsisch*</note>, that which is taken from the writings of Paracelsus or agrees with his opinion.
**Paradise** <note>original: *Paradisus*</note>, a pleasure-garden, the flourishing of heavenly powers, pure and free from the curse.

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical dictionary of "Theosophical and Latin words," moving further into the letter P. **Theosophical and Latin Words. Page 31**

**Paraphrasis** <note>original: *Paraphrasis*</note>, a somewhat detailed interpretation of obscure words and speeches.
**Part** <note>original: *Pars, Part*</note>, the part; **Parts** <note>original: *Partes*</note>, the parts; **By part** <note>original: *Parte*</note>, in the part.
**Particular**, piece-by-piece; also, that which works piece-by-piece. In Chemistry <note>original: *Chymie* - here referring to alchemy</note>, a **Particular** refers to a tincture that transforms only one part of a metal; likewise, one that only acts upon a specific metal but cannot affect others; whereas by contrast, that which acts upon all metals is called "Universal."
**Particularly** <note>original: *Particulariter*</note>, individually, especially.
**Pasquill**, a lampoon or libelous writing.
**Passions** <note>original: *Passiones*</note>, stirrings of the soul <note>original: *Gemüths-Regungen*</note> that arise from the love or anger of nature.
**To Pass** <note>original: *Passiren*</note>, to go by, to travel through.
**Patient**, a sufferer, a sick person, in body or soul.
**Patron** <note>original: *Patronus*</note>, a benefactor, one who takes the place of a father.
**To Pause** <note>original: *Pausiren*</note>, to stop while singing, to keep still.
**To Penetrate** <note>original: *Penetriren*</note>, to pierce through, to comprehend with the understanding, to grasp correctly.
**Persia**, a kingdom in Asia, beyond Turkey <note>original: *Türckey* - at the time of writing, this referred to the Ottoman Empire</note>.
**Fantasy** <note>original: *Phantasia*</note>, imagination, vain thoughts.
**Philautia**, self-love. <note>From the Greek *philautia* (φιλαυτία), often used in a negative sense to mean excessive egoism.</note>
**Philosophy** <note>original: *Philosophia*</note>, love of wisdom; also, worldly wisdom in all its parts and sciences; **Philosopher** <note>original: *Philosophus*</note>, one who possesses such wisdom, a "world-sage."
**To Philosophize**, to rationally reflect upon natural wisdom.
**Philosophical**, in the manner of the world-sages.
**Phlegm** <note>original: *Phlegma*</note>, a watery moisture.
**Phlegmatic**, of a watery, moist nature; it is one of the four temperaments <note>original: *Complexionen*</note> or qualities of human nature. <note>According to the ancient theory of the four humors, a phlegmatic person was thought to be calm, cool, and perhaps sluggish.</note>
**Phoenix**, may be a secret of nature which the ancients depicted as a bird of incomparable beauty, having no equal and thus living unpaired. It is said to nourish itself only on the pure dew of heaven, and at a great age, to build a nest of the finest spices; after this nest is ignited by the sun's rays, the bird burns itself within it, only to come alive again from the ashes after some time has passed.

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical index of terms, proceeding through the letter P. It follows the entry for "Philautia" (self-love) from the previous page. ### . P. First Register of

**Phrasis** <note>original: *Phrasis*</note>, phrases, modes of speaking.
**Physicist** <note>original: *Physicus*</note>, a natural scientist who investigates all kinds of <unclear>visible</unclear> bodies and whatever may be observed regarding them as far as <unclear>possible</unclear>; **Physicists** <note>original: *Physici*</note>, natural scientists; **physically** <note>original: *physice*</note>, physical, pertaining to natural science.
**Planets** <note>original: *Planeta*</note>, wandering stars that do not maintain a constant <unclear>position</unclear> but always move forward; they also have <unclear>no</unclear> light of their own that shines outward, but rather receive it from the S<unclear>un</unclear>. Seven of them are counted. See the [main] Register.
**Podagra**, gout in the feet.
**Poet** <note>original: *Poëta*</note>, a poet, writer, or maker of verses.
**Penalty** <note>original: *Pœna*</note>, punishment.
**Penitence** <note>original: *Pœnitentia*</note>, penance, repentance for sins.
**Policy** <note>original: *Policey*</note>, worldly or civil government, city and [state] order. <note>In this period, "Policey" referred to the general administration and well-ordered state of a community, rather than just law enforcement.</note>
**Politician** <note>original: *Politicus*</note>, a man of the world knowledgeable in matters of state.
**Pole** <note>original: *Polus*</note>, the pivot of the heavens; called by mathematicians two points on the depicted celestial sphere around which it appears to turn every 24 hours, as if on an axis. One is called the North Pole and the other the South Pole.
**Pomp** <note>original: *Pompa*</note>, splendor, state, a public and magnificent procession.
**To Ponder** <note>original: *Ponderiren*</note>, to weigh or consider.
**Port** <note>original: *Porta*</note>, the gate or door.
**Power** <note>original: *Potentia*</note>, might or force.
**Practice** <note>original: *Practica*</note>, exercise or application.
**To Practice** <note>original: *Practiciren*</note>, to exercise; also, to serve the sick as a physician.
**Practitioner** <note>original: *Practicus*</note>, one who is practiced and experienced; a **Medical Practitioner** <note>original: *Practicus Medicinæ*</note> is one authorized to publicly visit and cure the sick.
**Praxis**, exercise or experience.
**Prebend** <note>original: *Præbende*</note>, a benefice; the annual income from an <unclear>endowment</unclear> intended for the maintenance of church servants.
**Preceptor** <note>original: *Præceptor*</note>, a teacher or schoolmaster.
**Predestination** <note>original: *Prædestination*</note>, providence; the choice of God concerning mankind.
**To Predestinate** <note>original: *Prædestiniren*</note>, to foresee or elect beforehand.
**Preacher** <note>original: *Prædicant*</note>, a minister.
**To Predict** <note>original: *Prædiciren*</note>, to foretell or announce.
**Predictions** <note>original: *Prædicta*</note>, things told beforehand.
