### Theosophical and Latin Words.

<term>Preeminence</term> <note>original: *Præminenz*</note>, priority, excellence.
<term>Preface</term> <note>original: *Præfatio*</note>, the introduction.
<term>Prefigure</term> <note>original: *Præfiguriren*</note>, to foreshadow or model beforehand.
<term>Prepare</term> <note>original: *Præpariren*</note>, to prepare, to make ready.
<term>Preservation</term> <note>original: *Præservation*</note>, foresight, the protection of a thing from harm and misfortune.
<term>Presuppose</term> <note>original: *Præsupponiren*</note>, to assume, to require as a prior condition.
<term>Prime matter</term> <note>original: *Prima materia*</note>, the first matter; the very first thing required when something is to become actual. <note>In alchemy and theosophy, this refers to the formless base of all substance.</note>
<term>Primary</term> <note>original: *Primarius*</note>, the head pastor.
<term>Primate</term> <note>original: *Primas*</note>, the highest one.
<term>Primacy</term> <note>original: *Primatus*</note>, the highest power, top position.
<term>Prime</term> <note>original: *Primus*</note>, the first, the highest.
<term>Principal</term> <note>original: *Principal*</note>, the most noble; the master in whose service one stands.
<term>Principle</term> <note>original: *Principium*</note>, principles, the start, beginning; also a world, a birth, a new life. Thus, the 1st principle is the dark fire-world; the 2nd is the light- and love-world; the 3rd is the visible outer world, and all three are within one another, in all things. <note>This defines the "Three Principles" of Jacob Boehme’s cosmology, where different "worlds" or dimensions of existence coexist within the same space.</note>
<term>Privately</term> <note>original: *Privatim*</note>, secretly, especially, alone.
<term>Proof</term> <note>original: *Proba*</note>, the test, investigation, evidence, or validation.
<term>Probation</term> <note>original: *Probatio*</note>, the proof, confirmation, or approval.
<term>To prove</term> <note>original: *Probiren*</note>, to test, try, demonstrate, or approve.
<term>Process</term> <note>original: *Processus*</note>, the guidance of souls through the fire of temptation and purification, which has its stages; also the manner in which one proceeds in chemical and other works; also a legal dispute before courts.
<term>Profession</term> <note>original: *Profession*</note>, any calling in which one stands, an office, or a public teaching position at the universities.
<term>Professor</term> <note>original: *Professor*</note>, a public teacher for students.
<term>Profit</term> <note>original: *Profitiren*</note>, to make use of.
<term>Proportion</term> <note>original: *Proportion*</note>, the relationship of things to one another, the right size and shape.
<term>Proportioned</term> <note>original: *Proportioniret*</note>, well-formed, arranged according to number, measure, and weight; skillful.
<term>Personal genius</term> <note>original: *Proprius genius*</note>, one's own natural spirit, the innate character.

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical index of terms, moving from the letter P into the letter Q. It maintains the previous page's focus on defining Latinate terminology through the lens of theosophy and Jacob Boehme's mystical philosophy.</meta>

->P. Q. First Index of<-

<term>Own</term> <note>original: *Proprium*</note>, that which is characteristic or belonging to oneself.
<term>Protest</term> <note>original: *Proteſtiren*</note>, to publicly testify against a matter, to show that one is not satisfied with it and has objections to raise.
<term>Psalm</term> <note>original: *Pſalmus*</note>, a song or hymn, specifically the songs composed by David.
<term>Psychology</term> <note>original: *Pſychologia*</note>, the doctrine or study of the soul.
<term>True Psychology</term> <note>original: *Pſychologia vera*</note>, the true doctrine of the soul.
<term>Supplement to Psychology</term> <note>original: *Pſychologiæ Supplementum*</note>, an appendix or addition to the doctrine of the soul.
<term>Publish</term> <note>original: *Publiciren*</note>, to make known and understood; to reveal.
<term>Point</term> <note>original: *Punctum*</note>, a dot, a tittle <note>referring to a small mark in writing</note>, a specific part of a doctrine; also a moment or instant, or the center of a circle.
<term>Pure</term> <note>original: *Pur*</note>, clean, clear, alone, or sheer.
<term>Purgation</term> <note>original: *Purgatio*</note>, cleansing or justification <note>in a spiritual or legal sense of clearing oneself</note>.
<term>Purgatory</term> <note>original: *Purgatorium*</note>, the cleansing fire; the process of purification through fire.
<term>Putrefaction</term> <note>original: *Putrefaction*</note>, rotting or decay.
<term>Putrefy</term> <note>original: *Putreficiren*</note>, to rot or decompose.

## Q

<term>Square</term> <note>original: *Quadratus*</note>, four-sided, a quadrate; in astrology, a "square aspect," which occurs when planets are separated by a quarter of the Zodiac—that is, by three signs.
<term>Question</term> <note>original: *Quæſtio*</note>, an inquiry.
<term>Theosophical Questions</term> <note>original: *Quæſtiones Theoſophicæ*</note>, inquiries concerning Divine mysteries.
* <term>Torment or Well-spring</term> <note>original: *Qual*, *Quaal*, or *Quall*</note>, the movement of properties or qualities. In the Light, it is the gentle rising of powers from the weaving of the Spirit within the essence of the Will; in the Darkness, it is the movement of painfulness. In common speech, it is called "torment." See this entry explained more fully in the 3rd Index. <note>Boehme uses the word "Qual" as a technical term, punning on the German words for torment (Qual) and source/well-spring (Quelle) to describe the dynamic tension of spiritual forces.</note>
<term>To Qualify</term> <note>original: *Qualificiren*</note>, to "qual" or well up; the state where properties stir and move themselves toward birth or growth.
<term>Qualifying</term> <note>original: *Qualificirung*</note>, the weaving and moving of qualities; their inherent mobility.
<term>Quality</term> <note>original: *Qualitas*</note>, the mobile life and activity within the—

## R

**Rabbi**, Rabbis, Master or teacher of the Law <note>referring to Jewish teachers of the Torah</note>.
**Raca** <note>original: *Racha*, a term of contempt found in the Gospel of Matthew</note>, worthless person; was a word of reproach among the Jews.
**Active** <note>original: *Ræbs*; a term denoting something particularly vigorous or stirring</note>, effective and mobile above all others, occurring when a source-spirit <note>a "Quell-Geist," one of the fundamental creative forces in Boehme's philosophy</note> holds the upper hand in qualifying or birthing, as for example, joy in a happy person.
**Ransom** <note>original: *Ranzioniren*</note>, to buy back, redeem, or liberate.
**Raphael** <note>Hebrew: "God heals"</note>, God is the Physician; is the name of an angel.
**Magical Rapture** <note>original: *Raptum magicum*</note>, the ecstasy of the soul within the opened ground of light; also, a certain magical, figurative representation.
**Reason** <note>original: *Ratio, raison*</note>, intellect, or a rational proof.
**Prescription** <note>original: *Recept*</note>, a set of instructions and orders for medicines that the pharmacist is to prepare for the specific use of the sick; also, a remedy against this or that illness.
**To Reduce** <note>original: *Reduciren*</note>, to lead back, to restore something to its former state.
**To Refer** <note>original: *Referiren*</note>, to report, recount, or inform.
**Reformation**, improvement, the re-establishment of a thing into its proper form; generally refers to the change in the otherwise customary religion.
**To Reform** <note>original: *Reformiren*</note>, to improve, to bring a thing back to its previous good state, or to make a change in religion.
**Reformer** <note>original: *Reformator*</note>, one who introduces a change in the religion or other matters customary until that point. It can also be a judge awakened by God for a corrupt economy <note>here meaning a system or divine order of things</note> who is to remedy the decline.

According to the coarseness, it is the skin on the body and the skull on the head. # S. First Register of [Theosophical Words]. P. 38

Childlikeness and sensitivity. It is the <unclear>B...</unclear> of the internal and external senses and understanding.
**Scilicet** <note>original: *Scilicet*; Latin for "namely" or "it is evident"</note>, I also thought; yes, behind oneself; also <unclear>principally</unclear>.
**Writer** <note>original: *Scribent*</note>, a scribe, author of a book.

**Sectarian** or **Sectarianish** <note>original: *Sectirer*, *Sectiriſch*</note>, one who seeks to create such a following or is devoted to one.

# Theosophical and Latin Words. P. 39
### C 4

**Zion** <note>original: *Sion*</note> was a mountain in the city of Jerusalem, upon which David's fortress was built, and subsequently Solomon’s Temple in Jerusalem; the kings of Judah were also buried there. See **Zion**.

**Emerald** <note>original: *Smaragd*</note> is a green gemstone.

* **The Sun** <note>original: *Sol*</note>, is the 4th property of nature, the heart of the life of all properties and the cause of all nature-spirits (except for **Saturn** <note>original: *Saturno*; in this system, Saturn represents contraction and coldness</note>, which is a cause of physicality), just as it also has its seat in the heart of man. With the renewal in man, it creates a mind for itself in which it becomes **tinctural** <note>referring to a "tincture," a spiritual essence that can transform or "color" other substances</note>; it then re-opens the powers of the "spoken word" out of death and hiddenness in the heart. However, it is not a "birthing" of powers, since the outer **Archaeus** <note>a Paracelsian term for the vital formative principle of nature</note> or **Spirit of the World** <note>original: *Spir. M.* or *Spiritus Mundi*</note> is unstable <note>original: *unfix*</note>; what it builds, it breaks apart again, whereby the mind remains a mixed chaos of good and evil, entirely unstable. Therefore, a deeper foundation than that of the Sun must be laid; the spirit must strike the right fire in the soul, where it comes through God the Father to Christ. In metals, the Sun is **Gold**, and its sign is ☉.

**Hungarian Gold** <note>original: *Sol Hungariæ*</note>.

**Tincture of the Sun** <note>original: *Solis Tinctura*</note>, the tincture of the Sun, of Gold, and of the Heart. See **Sol**.

**Solar** <note>original: *Solarisch*</note>, whatever is of the nature and power of the Sun or of Gold.

* **Sophia** <note>Greek for "Wisdom"</note>, the **Wisdom**, is God’s revelation and the physicality of the Holy Spirit, the outflowed word of divine power, knowledge <note>original: *Wissenschaft*</note>, and holiness; the divine "found-ness" and sensitivity, in which the infinite, manifold, and distinct (or dispersed) powers of the **knowledge** <note>original: *Scienz*</note> of the spoken word exist in a **balanced temperament** <note>original: *Temperatur*; meaning a state where all qualities are perfectly harmonized</note>. She is the essential power of the love of God, from which all things have their movement and possibility. She is a passive being and a housing for the working of the love of God and of all humility, which is transmuted equally with the balance. Therefore, she is God’s **Throne of Grace** in man, and a mediator between God [and man...]

<meta>The catchword at the bottom of the page is "GOtt" (God), indicating the next page begins with this word, likely continuing the definition of Sophia as a mediator between God and humanity.</meta>

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical index of terms beginning with the letter "S." It follows the previous page's entries which ended with "Special." # Index of Theosophical and Latin Words. p. 41
### Sig. c 5

**Holy Spirit** <note>original Latin: *Spiritus Sanctus*</note>, the Holy Spirit, the Spirit of the 2nd Principle <note>In the author's "Three Principles" of divine manifestation, the Second Principle is the world of Light and the Son, which transcends the "First Principle" of raw natural fire</note>, **God** above nature.
**To Stipulate** <note>original: *Statuiren*</note>, to hold an opinion, to mean, to establish firmly.
**Status**, the standing, condition.
**Statutes**, laws, ordinances, magisterial commands.
**Study** <note>original: *Studium*</note>, diligence, reflection, and research into a matter.
**Study of Wisdom** <note>original Latin: *Studium Sapientiæ*</note>, zealous desire and love for wisdom.
**Style** <note>original: *Stylus*</note>, the manner of writing, how one is accustomed to expressing their thoughts in writing.
**Subjection** <note>original Latin: *Subjectio*</note>, submission, humiliation.
**Subject** <note>original Latin: *Subjectum*</note>, that which is being spoken of or treated; also, a person with whom a matter is being discussed.
\* **Substance** <note>original: *Substantia*</note>, essence; in the spirit, it is the enclosure <note>The "containing" or "forming" aspect</note> of the powers of the Speaking Word <note>The *Verbum Fiat* or creative Word of God</note>, from which heavenly corporeality—the blood and water of the souls—arises. This enclosure occurs through the thin will of faith in the Holy Spirit. However, the dark desire of nature impresses <note>The process of "Introduction" or "Impression" where spiritual desire becomes physical density</note> itself, making itself coarse, dark, and hard; the soul must depart from this "impression" again and draw a new will in the light, denying all nature if it wishes to work with **God**.
**Substantial**, essential, graspable.
**Subtle**, delicate, thin.
\* **Sulfur** <note>original Latin: *Sulphur*</note>, <note>alchemical symbol for sulfur: a triangle surmounting a cross</note>, Sulfur, <note>alchemical symbol for sulfur</note>, is in the spirit an essence that divides into two properties according to light and darkness: as *Sul* in joy, and as *Phur* in sorrow <note>A characteristic theosophical folk-etymology: *Sul* represents the light-flame and *Phur* the fiery-anguish</note>. It is the great burning of anguish in the soul until the light rises and separates itself from the darkness. Then each property stands within itself and one is the cause of the other, but they no longer mix.
**Sulfurous**, sulfur-like.
**Sum** <note>original: *Summa*</note>, the total, the content, everything together.
**Summaries**, a brief concept, the contents of a book or chapter.
**Summary**, briefly phrased.
**Superintendent**, a chief overseer, inspector, or bishop, under whose supervision a certain number of other church ministers and the church administration stand.

<summary>This page defines various Latinate and theosophical terms, focusing heavily on the spiritual-chemical definitions of Substance and Sulfur as dualistic forces of light and darkness. It provides insight into the author's view of how spiritual will becomes physical "impression" or "corporeality."</summary>
<keywords>Holy Spirit, Second Principle, Substantia, Sulfur, alchemical symbols, Speaking Word, heavenly corporeality, church hierarchy</keywords>

# First Index of Terms. p. 42
### Sig. c 5

**Supplication** <note>original Latin: *Supplicatio*</note>, a petition or written request from subjects to their superiors.
**Supra** <note>original Latin</note>, above, or mentioned previously.
**Syllable** <note>original Latin: *Syllaba*</note>, a part of a word; as many letters as one can pronounce naturally in a single breath while reading. Throughout his work, the author explains the **Natural Language** <note>The *Lingua Adamica*, a mystical concept where the sound and form of syllables reveal the inner essence or "signature" of the thing named</note> based on the syllables of the words encountered.
**Symbol** <note>original Latin: *Symbolum*</note>, a motto; a specific saying that one prefers and uses above others. The author favored this one in his letters: *Our salvation in the life of Jesus Christ within us.*
**Synagogue** <note>original Greek: *Synagoga*</note>, an assembly or Jewish school.
**Syria**, a region of Asia, currently under the dominion of the Turks <note>A reference to the Ottoman Empire's control of the Levant during the 17th century</note>.

->T<-

**Table** <note>original Latin: *Tabula*</note>, a chart; a brief and clearly arranged draft of a matter, so that one can oversee the entire context at once.
**Talent** <note>original Latin: *Talentum*</note>, the entrusted gift of nature or grace. Among the Jews, it was a hundredweight of silver according to the weight of the Sanctuary.
**Tantum** <note>original Latin</note>, enough of this; also: only so much, or so far.
**We praise thee, O God** <note>original Latin: *Te Deum laudamus*</note>, Lord God, we praise thee!
**Temperament** <note>original Latin: *Temperamentum*</note>, the right measure, the correct order and form of a thing; also the "naturell," the innate character and inclination of a human being.
**Temperance** <note>original Latin: *Temperantia*</note>, moderation or "temperature"; when love and wrath stand in balance <note>In this mystical system, "wrath" is the raw fire of nature and "love" is the light of God; temperance is the harmonious state where neither overwhelms the other</note>, the delight of the spirit, peace in the soul, and moderation.
**To Temper** <note>original German: *Temperiren*</note>, to bring to balance, to moderate, or to still.
**Tempering** <note>original German: *Temperirung*</note>, moderation, the act of equalizing, or formation.
**Term** <note>original Latin: *Terminus*</note>, a boundary or goal. Also, a word that certain scholars or artists specifically use; a technical term.
**Ternary** <note>original Latin: *Ternarius*</note>, the number three.
\* **Holy Ternary** <note>original Latin: *Ternarius Sanctus*</note>, this is God in Trinity and Wisdom above nature; the seat and bridal chamber of the **Virgin Sophia** <note>In theosophy, Sophia represents Divine Wisdom, the mirror of God and the "heavenly corporeality"</note>, the Holy of Holies of the Spirit.

# Theosophical and Latin Words. T.

**Testament** <note>original Latin: *Testamentum*</note>, a legacy, the last will, the last decree of a dying person, etc.; a covenant.
**Testator** <note>original Latin</note>, one who bequeaths or establishes something.
**Tetragrammaton** <note>Greek: "word of four letters"</note>, the word of 4 letters. This is what the Jews call the Holy Name of God יהוה <note>The four Hebrew letters Yod, He, Vav, He, often rendered as Yahweh or Jehovah</note> **JeHoVaH**, which they have not been permitted to read or pronounce since ancient times, but instead say only "the Word" <note>Referencing the practice of substituting *Adonai* (Lord) or *HaShem* (The Name) to avoid profaning the Tetragrammaton</note>. Which manner of speaking John the Evangelist retained in chapter 1, verse 14 <note>"And the Word was made flesh..."</note>. The author has likewise fully expressed it in the light.
**Teutonic** <note>original Latin: *Teutonicus*</note>, German; a German person <note>A reference to the author, Jacob Böhme, who was known as the *Philosophus Teutonicus* or the "Teutonic Philosopher"</note>.
**Theodidactus** <note>Greek/Latin: "God-taught"</note>, one who is learned in God.
**Theology** <note>original Latin: *Theologia*</note>, scholarship in the Scripture; the science of a religion derived from the Scripture in all its parts, including external practice; as it is taught in schools.
**Theologian** <note>original Latin: *Theologus*</note>, a scriptural scholar who has learned his religion in detail from the Scripture in schools, and is capable of instructing others in it and administering public church customs.
**Theo-philosopher** <note>Greek/Latin: *Theo-philosophus*</note>, a wise man enlightened by God.
**Theophrastus**, see **Paracelsus** <note>Referring to the famous physician and alchemist Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim, whose work influenced Böhme</note>.
**Theosophy** <note>original Latin: *Theosophia*</note>, divine wisdom.
**Theosophia revelata** <note>Latin</note>, revealed divine wisdom.
**Theosophical**, according to divine wisdom.
**Theosophist** <note>original Latin: *Theosophus*</note>, one enlightened in the mysteries of divine wisdom.
**Theoscopia** <note>Greek/Latin: "contemplation of God"</note>, divine contemplativeness.
**Theriac**, a common medicine against poison; an antidote.
**Thesaurus** <note>Latin</note>, a treasure.
**Thesaurinella** <note>Latin diminutive</note>, a core or "little treasury" of all treasures.
† **Tincture** <note>original Latin: *Tinctura*. In alchemy, a substance capable of transforming metals; in Böhme’s thought, it is the spiritual "soul-blood" or life-force that penetrates and transfigures all things</note>, is a thing that separates and brings the pure from the impure. It is the life of all spirits and brings all essences to their highest degree. Its true name is wonderful, and no one can name it except him to whom it is given: he names it only within himself, not outside of himself. In the heavenly birth of life, it is the second re-contained thin light-

# T. First Index of the

<meta>The following text is the conclusion of the entry for "Tincture" from the preceding page.</meta>

...The will of the soul, which proceeds out of the dark impression and manifests itself with the heart of God in faith; therein, in the Tincture, the Virgin—Christ’s heavenly humanity, the new body of our soul—rises up, with which the soul is clothed. She is the greening of the spirit out of the death of the dark, coarse essence, a flower of eternal life, acting as the divine, lovely delight of our spirit, in which the joy of eternal life rises up without the shouting and exaltation of nature <note>In Böhme’s thought, "nature" often refers to the self-centered, turbulent driving force of existence which must be calmed by divine light</note>.

**Tingeing** <note>original: *Tingiren*; from Latin *tingere*</note>, to dye, etc.; to transform a base metal into a nobler one by means of the Tincture opened within it <note>An alchemical metaphor for spiritual transformation</note>.

**Title** <note>original Latin: *Titulus*</note>, the heading of a book; a title of honor.

***Turba*** <note>Latin: "turmoil" or "disturbance"</note>, the stirred-up grimness and wrath in nature; a poisonous quality that shatters everything good. (This is to be understood primarily in relation to the mind or disposition.)

***Turba magna*** <note>Latin: "the great disturbance"</note>, the ignited wrath of God in nature; judgments upon the world.

**To Turbate** <note>original: *Turbiren*; from Latin *turbare*</note>, to confuse, to disturb.

**Treatise** <note>original Latin: *Tractatus*</note>, a tract, a writing, a discussion, a book.

**To Treat** <note>original: *Tractiren*; from Latin *tractare*</note>, to discuss or handle a subject.

**Tradition** <note>original Latin: *Traditio*</note>, an oral report that has been propagated from ancient times, from generation to generation.

***Traduce*** <note>original Latin: *ex traduce*, meaning "from a branch"</note>, through the propagation of the seed <note>Refers to the theological concept of Traducianism, where the soul is transmitted from parents to children through natural generation</note>.

**To Transform** <note>original: *Transformiren*; from Latin *transformare*</note>, to bring into another shape and form.

**Transmutation** <note>original Latin: *Transmutatio*</note>, a transformation or change.

**To Transmute** <note>original: *Transmutiren*; from Latin *transmutare*</note>, to transform, to change.

**Triangle** <note>original Latin: *Triangulus*</note>, a three-cornered shape; a figure that has 3 sides and 3 angles.

**Tribulation** <note>original Latin: *Tribulatio*</note>, a plague, anxiety, or oppression.

***Tribus*** <note>Latin</note>, of three.

**Trinity** <note>original Latin: *Trinitas*</note>, threeness, Tri-unity, or threefoldness.

***Triplici*** <note>Latin</note>, of the threefold.

***Triumph*** <note>original Latin: *Triumphus*</note>, in the spirit, this is what it is called when love has conquered over wrath; when the heavenly Virgin gives birth to the essences of spirit, soul, and mind in the highest light, and walks forth in the spirit itself, the

In the spirit it signifies when one is occupied with a thing that makes the mind impure, bringing doubt, temptation, accusation, and strife: when one raises such a dark <term>Ens</term> <note>Latin for "being" or "essence"</note> up with faith, and presents oneself with it in the Holy Element before <term>God</term>, for the separation of light and darkness, through which one is rid of the evil in the mind. Therefore it is said in Psalm 1: the ungodly (the evil <term>Ens</term>) do not stand in judgment, but are like the chaff which the wind scatters.

# V. First Index of the
<meta>The text begins mid-word, continuing from the previous page's discussion of human nature.</meta>

...[humanity] was poisoned by Satan, that ancient serpent, at the fall of the first parents: whereby out of the modest desire for love, a false earthly whoredom <note>original: *Huren-be...*; likely *Hurenbegierde* or *Hurenbeiwohnung*, referring to lustful or "whorish" desire</note> arose. From which shame **God** cleanses them in the newborn children of **Christ** with fire. In the outer man, her star <note>In theosophical astrology, planets are often referred to as "stars" that influence the human body</note> is in the kidneys. She has her own light among all stars. In metals, she is copper, and her symbol is ♀.

**Venereal**, that which is of the aforementioned nature <note>referring to the qualities of Venus described above</note>.
**The Word** <note>original Latin: *Verbum*</note>. See in the 3rd Index.
**The Word of God** <note>original Latin: *Verbum Dei*</note>, the Son of **God**.

**Vision**, a sight or apparition.
**Life** <note>original Latin: *Vita*</note>.
**Life of the Author** <note>original Latin: *Vita Autoris*</note>, the life or biography of the writer.
**Vowel** <note>original Latin: *Vocalis*</note>, a letter that can be pronounced without the addition of another, and which makes the word audible; there are 5 of these: a, e, i, o, u. The others are called **Consonants**, and cannot be pronounced unless a vowel is added.
**Vocation**, the calling or call.
**As above** <note>original Latin: *Ut supra*</note>, as previously mentioned.
**Vulcan**, the fire, which the heathens honored as divine in all professions that work with fire.

<meta>This page concludes the first alphabetical index of terms, moving from the end of the V entries (continued from the previous page's "Vici" or "I have overcome") to the final letter, Z. # Theosophical and Latin Words. Z.
<meta>catchword: The</meta>

->Z<-

**Zion** is a paradisiacal essence and the greening of the new body in **Christ**, arising out of the parched soil of nature within us: a dry place <note>original: *dürrer Ort*; Zion is etymologically often linked to the Hebrew word for "dryness" or "parched ground," which the author here contrasts with the spiritual "greening" of the soul</note>.

**Sabaoth** <note>original: *Zebaoth*</note>, hosts, or an angelic multitude. Therefore **God** is called **God Sabaoth**, or the **God of Hosts**—that is, of many armies of angels.

**Zodiac** <note>original Latin: *Zodiacus*</note>, the Circle of Animals <note>original: *Thier-Kreiß*</note> in the heavens; this is the name for that region of the sky where we see the sun throughout the entire year, where the ancient sages indicated the stars through images and figures of animals. This was because the stars only influence the animal or outer soul-spirit—which all animals possess—and bring forth animalistic qualities. There are 12 such starry images in this celestial region, according to the 12 months of the year, as is well known from calendars.

### Micah.

<margin>7 | 1</margin> It is with me as with one who gleans in the vineyard, <term>Three Principles 12:46, 20:55.</term> <term>Apology 2 against Tilke 133.</term> <term>Election of Grace 11:20.</term> <note>This verse describes the prophet's soul-searching and the scarcity of the upright.</note>

### Zechariah.

<margin>1 | 3</margin> Turn to me, says the Lord of Hosts, <note>Original: "Zebaoth," a Hebrew title for God as the leader of heavenly armies.</note> <term>Apology 2 against Tilke 110.</term>

<margin>7 | 5</margin> Wisdom is the breath of Divine power, <term>Clavis 17.</term> <note>Original "Clav." refers to *The Key*, a short guide to Böhme's terminology.</note>
<margin>11 | 22</margin> You have ordered all things with measure, number, and weight, <term>Signatura Rerum 8:3.</term> <term>Election of Grace 7:51, 10:21, 12:51.</term>
<margin>16 | 20. 21</margin> In contrast, you nourished your people with the food of angels, <term>Three Principles 24:31.</term>

<margin>3 | 20</margin> The higher you are, the more humble yourself, <term>Apology 1 against Tilke 630.</term>

<margin>14 | 39-41</margin> Of the drinking cup of Sophia, <term>Election of Grace 3:21.</term> <note>Sophia is the Greek word for Wisdom, personified in Böhme's work as the "mirror" of God or the heavenly bride of the soul.</note>
